[^1437]: Physical leaf p210 (Fol. 104v). First Sticheron of St. Josaphat at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs", containing the petition for the union of the Churches.

[^1438]: Physical leaf p210 (Fol. 104v). Second Sticheron of St. Josaphat at "Lord, I have cried".

[^1439]: The Church Slavonic text reads `невѣ́твержденнаѧ`, an epigraphic haplographic ligature or typo for `неоу҆твержде́ннаѧ` ("unconfirmed" or "unestablished").

[^1440]: "A twofold crown" (`сꙋгꙋ́бъ вѣне́цъ`), referring to the double crown of hierarch and martyr.

[^1441]: "Worshiper of Jesus the Almighty" (`чести́тель бы́въ їи҃са вседержи́телѧ`).

[^1442]: The printed text contains the typographical error `мꙋ́никъ` for `мꙋ́ченикъ` (`Ꙗ҆́кѡ мꙋ́никъ бо просвѣща́емь`).

[^1443]: Doxastikon of the Saint at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 2: "A lawful priest wast thou..." (`Сщ҃е́нникъ зако́ненъ`).

[^1444]: Recensional witness: The 1761 Pochaiv Menaion preserves the royal petition for the reigning Polish monarch Augustus III: "and the horn of our faithful King is exalted together with it" (`и҆ ро́гъ вѣ́рнагѡ совозно́ситсѧ королѧ̀ на́шегѡ`).

[^1445]: Liturgical composite reading from Proverbs: composite of LXX Prov. 10:7, 6; 3:13–16; 8:32, 35, 4, 12, 14, 17; 8:5–10.

[^1446]: Liturgical composite reading from the Wisdom of Solomon: composite of Prov. 29:2; Wis. 4:1, 10; 6:21, 12, 17–18, 21–22; 7:15–16, 22, 26, 27, 29; 10:9–10, 12; 7:30; 1:8; 2:1, 10–17, 19–20, 21–22; 16:13, 15.

[^1447]: Scriptural reference: Wis. 4:20; 5:1–7.

[^1448]: Scriptural citations: LXX Ps. 98:5, 131:7; 16:8; 4:7.

[^1449]: Sticheron at Litiya for Saint Josaphat: "With divine sacred praises..." (`Сщ҃енносла́вїи бж҃твенными`).

[^1450]: Sticheron at Litiya for Saint Josaphat: "Having ministered the divine Gospel..." The text specifically references Josaphat enriching the Kyivan archeparchial see (`то́къ же кїе́вскїй оу҆добри́лъ є҆сѝ`).

[^1451]: Sticheron at Litiya for Saint Josaphat: "A ladder of ascent..." (`Восхо́дный степе́нь, сꙋгꙋ́бь стѧжа̀ сщ҃енныѧ ти по́двиги`).

[^1452]: Doxastikon of the Saint at Litiya, Tone 5: "This thy radiant solemnity..." (`Свѣтоно́сное твое́ сїѐ торжество̀`).

[^1453]: Both now at Litiya, Tone 5: "Come, ye peoples, seeing the most glorious miracle..." (`Прїиди́те лю́дїе пресла́вное чꙋ́до ви́дѧще`).

[^1454]: Sticheron at the Aposticha for Saint Josaphat, Tone 4: "Like a radiant sun..." (`Ꙗ҆́кѡ со́лнце многосвѣ́тлое`). The text highlights his labor for the "unity of the Orthodox Catholic Church" (`къ соединенїю це́ркве правосла́вныѧ каѳолі́ческїѧ приве́лъ`).

[^1455]: Sticheron at the Aposticha for Saint Josaphat: "Equal in honor to the Apostles..." (`А҆пл҃ѡмъ равноче́стный бы́лъ є҆сѝ`).

[^1456]: Sticheron at the Aposticha for Saint Josaphat: "Offering an honorable ministry..." (`Слꙋ́жбꙋ приносѧ̀ чтнꙋ́ю`). Allusion to Christ's High Priestly Prayer in John 17:11, 21: "that they all may be one" (`да бꙋ́дꙋтъ є҆ди́но всѝ`).

[^1457]: Doxastikon at the Aposticha for Saint Josaphat, Tone 4: "Thy good works have shone like the sun..." (`Просїѧ́ша дѡ́браѧ дѣла̀ твоѧ̑`).

[^1458]: Both now at the Aposticha, of the Feast, Tone 8: "The Cross which Moses of old prefigured..." (`Є҆́гоже дре́вле мѡѷсе́й проѡбрази́въ`).

[^1459]: Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4: "A radiant beacon didst thou appear..." (`Свѣти́лникъ свѣ́тлый ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ сщ҃енномꙋ́чениче їѡсафа́те`).

[^1460]: Incipit of the Festal Troparion of the Exaltation of the Holy Cross: "O Lord, save Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance..." (`Спасѝ гдⷭ҇и лю́ди твоѧ̑...`).

[^1461]: Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma at Matins, Tone 8: "Having forsaken all earthly things..." The physical print has `неочи́стѧщыѧ` (for `неѡчи́стныѧ` passions).

[^1462]: Both now after the 1st Kathisma, of the Feast, Tone 8: "Joshua, the son of Nun, mystically prefigured..." (`Проѡбража́ше та́инственнѡ дре́вле і҆исꙋ́съ навѵ́нъ`).

[^1463]: Sessional Hymn of the Saint after the 2nd Kathisma, Tone 4: "Having made thy heart a fair altar..." (`Же́ртвенникъ кра́сенъ ср҃це твое́ соверши́вый`).

[^1464]: Both now after the 2nd Kathisma, of the Feast, Tone 4: "Moses, stretching out his hands..." (`Просте́ръ мѡѷсе́й рꙋ́ки`).

[^1465]: Magnification (`Велича́нїе`) of Saint Josaphat for the Polyeleos, with its appointed psalm verses.

[^1466]: Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 4: "Having cast down the haughtiness..." (`Низло́жь врагѡ́въ раздоротво́рныхъ возноше́нїа`).

[^1467]: Both now after the Polyeleos, of the Feast, Tone 8: "Of old the tree stripped me bare in paradise..." (`Въ раи́ мѧ пре́жде дре́во ѡ҆бнажѝ`).

[^1468]: Rubric for the Matins service order (Anavathmoi, Prokeimenon, Gospel pericope 35 from John 10:1–9, Psalm 50 sticheron).

[^1469]: Rubrics for the Canon of Matins.

[^1470]: Canon to the Hieromartyr Josaphat, Ode 1, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Come, ye peoples..." (`Грѧди́те лю́дїе`).

[^1471]: Ode 1, Troparion 1 of the Saint.

[^1472]: Ode 1, Troparion 2 of the Saint.

[^1473]: Ode 1, Troparion 3 of the Saint: the "twofold crown" of priesthood and martyrdom.

[^1474]: Ode 1, Theotokion.

[^1475]: Katavasia of the Cross, Ode 1, Tone 8: "Tracing a cross upright with his rod..." (`Кр҇тъ начерта́въ мѡѷсе́й`).

[^1476]: Ode 3, Tone 6, Heirmos: "Having established me upon the rock of faith..." (`На ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры оу҆тверди́въ`).

[^1477]: Ode 3, Troparion 1 of the Saint.

[^1478]: Ode 3, Troparion 2 of the Saint.

[^1479]: Ode 3, Troparion 3 of the Saint.

[^1480]: Ode 3, Theotokion.

[^1481]: Katavasia of the Cross, Ode 3, Tone 8: "The rod is accepted as a figure of the mystery..." (`Же́злъ во ѡ҆́бразъ та́йны прїе́млетсѧ`).

[^1482]: Kontakion of the Feast of the Exaltation, Tone 4: "Thou Who of Thine own will wast lifted up upon the Cross..." Note the royal petition for King Augustus III of Poland (`бл҃говѣ́рнаго королѧ̀ на́шего`).

[^1483]: Ikos of the Feast of the Exaltation, referring to St. Paul's vision in 2 Cor. 12:2–4 and Gal. 6:14.

[^1484]: Sessional Hymn of the Saint after Ode 3, Tone 4: "Thou didst burn up the deception of the schismatics..." (`Пре́лесть попали́лъ є҆сѝ`).

[^1485]: Sessional Hymn of the Feast, Tone 4: "In thee, O thrice-blessed and life-giving Cross..." (`Ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ требл҃же́нне и҆ жизнода́вче кр҇те`).

[^1486]: Ode 4, Tone 6, Heirmos: "I heard the report of Thy coming, O Christ..." (`Оу҆слы́шахъ слꙋ́хъ`).

[^1487]: Ode 4, Troparion 1 of the Saint. The physical print has `мꙋ́ченнческій` for `мꙋ́ченическій`.

[^1488]: Ode 4, Troparion 2 of the Saint.

[^1489]: Ode 4, Troparion 3 of the Saint.

[^1490]: Ode 4, Theotokion.

[^1491]: Katavasia of the Cross, Ode 4, Tone 8: "I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation..." (`Оу҆слы́шахъ гдⷭ҇и смотрѐнїѧ твоегѡ̀ та́инство...`).

[^1492]: Habakkuk 3:2. Katavasia of Ode 4, Tone 8, Feast of the Universal Exaltation of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross.

[^1493]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 6.

[^1494]: Church Slavonic: ѻ҆ско́рдомъ (*oskordom*) — a woodsman's axe or broad-headed hatchet, the weapon by which the anti-Union mob dealt St. Josaphat the mortal blow to his skull in Vitebsk on November 12, 1623.

[^1495]: Church Slavonic: въ соединені́и сꙋ́щихъ — those residing in the Union of Brest (1596), which restored communion between the Ruthenian Church and the Apostolic See of Rome.

[^1496]: Katavasia of Ode 5, Tone 8, Feast of the Exaltation of the Cross.

[^1497]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 6.

[^1498]: Church Slavonic: рѣкѝ дзви́ны — the Western Dvina (Dzvina) River in Vitebsk, into which the murderers dragged and cast the martyr's weighted body.

[^1499]: Church Slavonic: бѣлоросі́йскїй ѧ҆зы́къ — literally "the Belarusian tongue" or "the Belarusian nation," denoting the flock of the archeparchy of Polotsk, Vitebsk, and Mstislav.

[^1500]: Katavasia of Ode 6, Tone 8, Feast of the Exaltation of the Cross.

[^1501]: Church Slavonic: распролю́бныхъ распале́ннаѧ раздоростві́емъ сердца̀ — the hearts of the lovers of schism inflamed with discord.

[^1502]: Kontakion of St. Josaphat, Archbishop of Polotsk and Hieromartyr, Tone 4.

[^1503]: Ikos of St. Josaphat, structured with the classical twelve acclamatory strophes (*Rejoice...*).

[^1504]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 6 (Dan. 3:26–28).

[^1505]: Church Slavonic: плꙋчꙋ́нꙋ — variant or misprint for пꙋчи́нꙋ (*puchynu*, the stormy deep / abyss / surges).

[^1506]: Church Slavonic: раздоро́рства — printing error in the 1761 Pochaiv edition for раздоро́тства (*razdorotstva*, schism / factional strife).

[^1507]: Katavasia of Ode 7, Tone 8, Feast of the Exaltation of the Cross (Dan. 3:19–24).

[^1508]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 6 (Dan. 3:57).

[^1509]: Church Slavonic: пра́вилнымъ и҆звѣ́ствомъ — canonical certitude or synodal decree.

[^1510]: Katavasia of Ode 8, Tone 8, Feast of the Exaltation of the Cross.

[^1511]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 6.

[^1512]: Supplication for the Polish monarch (King Augustus III, r. 1734–1763) embedded within the Theotokion of Ode 9.

[^1513]: Katavasia of Ode 9, Tone 8, Feast of the Exaltation of the Cross.

[^1514]: Exapostilarion of St. Josaphat.

[^1515]: The classic Exapostilarion/Hymn to the Precious Cross (*Krest khranitel vseye vselenniya*).

[^1516]: LXX Psalm 82:3–4 (MT Ps. 83:2–3).

[^1517]: LXX Psalm 82:5 (MT Ps. 83:4).

[^1518]: Historical martyrdom of St. Josaphat Kuntsevych on November 12, 1623: after his head was split with an axe and he was shot, stones were bound to his neck and limbs and his body was dragged and hurled from Castle Hill into the Dvina River.

[^1519]: Sticheron at the Praises of the Exaltation, Tone 6.

[^1520]: Prokeimenon at the Liturgy: LXX Psalm 115:6 (MT Ps. 116:15).

[^1521]: LXX Psalm 115:3 (MT Ps. 116:12).

[^1522]: Epistle pericope for a Hieromartyr/Hierarch: Hebrews 7:26–8:2 (Pericope 334).

[^1523]: LXX Psalm 131:9.

[^1524]: LXX Psalm 91:13 (MT Ps. 92:12).

[^1525]: Gospel pericope for the Good Shepherd: John 10:9–16 (Pericope 36).

[^1526]: Kinonikon (Communion Verse): LXX Psalm 111:6b.

[^1527]: Feast of the Holy Martyr Sophia and her three virgin daughters: Faith (*Vera / Pistis*), Hope (*Nadezhda / Elpis*), and Love (*Lyubov / Agape*), martyred in Rome under the Emperor Hadrian (c. 137).

[^1528]: Festal Sticheron of the Exaltation of the Cross: Tone 1, model *Nebesnykh chinov*.

[^1529]: Exodus 15:23–25 (the tree cast into the bitter waters of Marah).

[^1530]: Tone 4 Prosomoion to the Holy Martyr Daughters of Sophia, model *Dal yesi znameniye*.

[^1531]: The names of the three virgin martyrs correspond to the three theological virtues (1 Cor. 13:13), born of Sophia (Divine Wisdom).

[^1532]: LXX Psalm 98:5. Idiomelon at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 6.

[^1533]: Aposticha Sticheron of the Exaltation, Tone 2, model *Dome Evfratov*.

[^1534]: Exodus 17:11–12.

[^1535]: Genesis 48:13–14 (Jacob blessing Ephraim and Manasseh with crossed hands).

[^1536]: Royal petition for the King of Poland (Augustus III) with typological reference to the vision of the Cross granted to Emperor Constantine the Great.

[^1537]: Troparion of the Exaltation of the Cross (*Spasi, Gospodi, lyudi Tvoya*), Tone 1.

[^1538]: Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma, Tone 2, model *Blagoutrobiya sushchi*.

[^1539]: Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma, Tone 4, model *Skoro predvari*.

[^1540]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation: Tone 8, Moses parting the Red Sea with his rod (Exod. 14:16, 21–28).

[^1541]: Exodus 17:8–13.

[^1542]: Numbers 21:8–9.

[^1543]: The heavenly vision of the Cross granted to Emperor Constantine the Great prior to the Battle of the Milvian Bridge (312).

[^1544]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon to the Holy Martyrs Sophia, Faith, Hope, and Love: Tone 1, by St. Theophanes the Branded (*Theophanovo*), with alphabetic acrostic in Greek.

[^1545]: Theological invocation of Christ the Wisdom of God (1 Cor. 1:24) in honor of St. Sophia and her daughters.

[^1546]: St. Sophia's name explicitly connected to divine wisdom (*philosophia* in the patristic sense).

[^1547]: The three virgin daughters of Sophia: Faith (*Pistis / Vera*), Hope (*Elpis / Nadezhda*), and Love (*Agape / Lyubov*).

[^1548]: Theotokion of Ode 1 (Phil. 2:6–7).

[^1549]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Feast of the Exaltation: Tone 8, Aaron's rod budding (Num. 17:8).

[^1550]: Exodus 17:6; Numbers 20:11.

[^1551]: John 19:34.

[^1552]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs: Tone 1, model *Yedine vyediy*.

[^1553]: Matthew 10:19–20; Luke 21:14–15.

[^1554]: The martyrdom of the young maidens Faith, Hope, and Love before the Roman prefect Antiochus and Emperor Hadrian.

[^1555]: Theotokion of Ode 3 (Isa. 57:15).

[^1556]: The Pochaiv print rubrics this Kontakion under Tone 4 while prescribing the automelon model *Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се* (*Thy Tomb, O Savior*), which in standard Byzantine and Slavonic hymnography belongs to Tone 1.

[^1557]: The automelon *Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй* (*The Angelic Choir*) belongs to Tone 1.

[^1558]: St. Paul's boast concerning the Cross of the Lord reflects Galatians 6:14.

[^1559]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross, quoting Habakkuk 3:2.

[^1560]: The sweetening of the bitter waters of Marah by the wood cast in by Moses (Exodus 15:23–25) is a classical patristic type of the Cross.

[^1561]: The miracle of the iron axe-head floating when the prophet Elisha threw a stick into the Jordan (2 Kings [4 Kingdoms] 6:5–7) prefigures both the wood of the Cross and baptismal renewal.

[^1562]: The fourfold encamped arrangement of the tribes of Israel around the Tabernacle (Numbers 2) was understood typologically as forming a cross.

[^1563]: Citing Psalm 18:2 [LXX Ps. 18:1]: "The heavens declare the glory of God."

[^1564]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1, invoking Habakkuk 3:3.

[^1565]: The three maiden daughters of Sophia—Faith (Pistis/Vira), Hope (Elpis/Nadezhda), and Love (Agape/Liubov)—suffered in Rome under Emperor Hadrian.

[^1566]: The three daughters are commemorated as embodying the three theological virtues (1 Corinthians 13:13).

[^1567]: Alluding to Genesis 49:10: "The expectation of the nations."

[^1568]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8.

[^1569]: Referring to the fiery turning sword placed to guard the way of the Tree of Life in Eden (Genesis 3:24).

[^1570]: Alluding to Philippians 2:10: "That at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth."

[^1571]: The Cross is praised as the divine instrument drawing the Gentiles unto the knowledge of Christ.

[^1572]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1.

[^1573]: The martyrdom of the holy virgin sisters before the Roman tribunal.

[^1574]: Eve the foremother is redeemed through the victory of the holy virgins over the ancient serpent.

[^1575]: The holy martyrs rejected the enticements and bribes of the judge, desiring only Christ.

[^1576]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, evoking the sign of Jonah (Jonah 2; Matthew 12:40).

[^1577]: The patriarch Jacob crossing his arms to bless Joseph's sons Ephraim and Manasseh (Genesis 48:14) is a classical prefiguration of the Cross.

[^1578]: Genesis 48:17–19, where Jacob preferentially blesses Ephraim, the younger, prefiguring the New Testament Church superseding the elder synagogue.

[^1579]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1, citing Psalm 43:23 [LXX Ps. 43:22].

[^1580]: The three sisters reflect the mystical number of the Holy Trinity.

[^1581]: The twofold crown refers to the crown of virginity and the crown of martyrdom.

[^1582]: Alluding to Psalm 44:15–16 [LXX Ps. 44:14–15]: "Virgins shall be brought unto the King."

[^1583]: The perpetual virginity of the Mother of God: virgin before, during, and after childbearing.

[^1584]: The Pochaiv text explicitly prints "нашего королѧ" (our King, referring to Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania), characteristic of the 18th-century Basilian Ruthenian recension, where synodal Moscow prints have "благоверному Императору нашему" (our faithful Emperor).

[^1585]: The Ikos brings together St. Paul's heavenly vision (2 Corinthians 12:2–4) and his confession of the Cross (Galatians 6:14).

[^1586]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, reflecting Daniel 3.

[^1587]: The tree of the knowledge of good and evil brought bodily corruption and death unto Adam and his descendants (Genesis 3:6, 19).

[^1588]: The Cherubim barred the way of the Tree of Life in Eden (Genesis 3:24), which was opened by Christ to the wise thief (Luke 23:42–43).

[^1589]: Genesis 47:31 and Hebrews 11:21: "By faith Jacob, when he was a dying, blessed both the sons of Joseph; and worshipped, leaning upon the top of his staff."

[^1590]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1.

[^1591]: The three martyrs confessed the consubstantial Trinity before their heathen tormentors.

[^1592]: The heavenly dwellings prepared for the holy virgin martyrs (John 14:2).

[^1593]: Like the three youths in the furnace of Babylon, the three maidens overcame the power of fire.

[^1594]: The Virgin Theotokos is praised as having held God in her embrace.

[^1595]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8.

[^1596]: The cosmic significance of the Exaltation of the Cross, uniting heaven and earth.

[^1597]: John 19:34: the piercing of Christ's side with a lance on the Cross.

[^1598]: The Cross is praised as the protector of Christian rulers and the scatterer of hostile nations.

[^1599]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1.

[^1600]: The three virgin martyrs are acclaimed as radiant witnesses to the resurrection.

[^1601]: Commemorating the bodily relics and shrines of the martyrs Faith, Hope, Love, and their mother Sophia.

[^1602]: The Mother of God is prefigured as the untilled earth that bore the bread of life.

[^1603]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8.

[^1604]: Citing Psalm 95:12 [LXX Ps. 95:12]: "Then shall all the trees of the wood rejoice before the Lord."

[^1605]: Alluding to Psalm 74:11 [LXX Ps. 74:10]: "All the horns of the ungodly also will I break, but the horns of the righteous shall be exalted."

[^1606]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of the Martyrs, Tone 1, citing the burning bush of Exodus 3:2.

[^1607]: An invocation of the namesakes of the theological virtues (1 Corinthians 13:13).

[^1608]: A prayer for unity in the faith and deliverance from heresies.

[^1609]: The martyrs passing from earthly darkness into the unending day of the kingdom of God.

[^1610]: The celebrated Festal Exapostilarion of the Precious Cross (*Крⷭ҇тъ храни́тель всеѧ̀ вселе́нныѧ*).

[^1611]: Evoking Psalm 131:7 [LXX Ps. 131:7]: "We will worship at the place where His feet stood."

[^1612]: Reflecting Romans 6:6 and Galatians 2:20: "I am crucified with Christ."

[^1613]: The water and blood flowing from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34).

[^1614]: The solemn festal Doxasticon at the Aposticha of the Exaltation, attributed in liturgical manuscripts to the Emperor Leo the Wise.

[^1615]: St. Eumenius, Bishop of Gortyna in Crete (7th century), renowned for his ascetic life, almsgiving, and wonderworking.

[^1616]: The Exaltation of the Cross is commemorated continuously throughout its post-festive afterfeast (September 15–21).

[^1617]: Evoking Psalm 98:5 and Psalm 131:7 [LXX Ps. 98:5; 131:7]: "Worship at His footstool."

[^1618]: The Cross is acclaimed as the throne and invincible defense of Christ's kingdom.

[^1619]: The printed Pochaiv rubric prescribes Tone 8 with the model "The same" (referring back to *As One Valiant among Martyrs* of Tone 4), an evident typographic anomaly in the original print.

[^1620]: St. Eumenius is praised as a fellow-citizen of the angels and citizen of the heavenly Jerusalem.

[^1621]: St. Eumenius was known for driving out demons and healing the sick through his prayers.

[^1622]: The miraculous vision of the Cross granted to Constantine the Great, leading to the recovery of the True Cross by his mother, St. Helena, in Jerusalem.

[^1623]: Aposticha Sticheron 1 of the Afterfeast, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha."

[^1624]: The Cross as the spiritual ladder lifting humanity from earth to heaven.

[^1625]: Citing Psalm 73:12 [LXX Ps. 73:12]: "God is our King of old, working salvation in the midst of the earth."

[^1626]: Exodus 17:11 and Psalm 103:24 [LXX Ps. 103:24]: "O Lord, how manifold are Thy works!"

[^1627]: The common Troparion of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross (*Спасѝ Го́споди лю́ди Твоя̑*), Tone 1.

[^1628]: Sessional Hymn after the 1st Kathisma reading, Tone 4, evoking Christ's triumph over the gates of hades (1 Peter 3:19; Colossians 2:14).

[^1629]: Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma reading, Tone 6, citing the piercing of Christ's side (John 19:34) and the canceling of the bond (Colossians 2:14).

[^1630]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross, Tone 8, by St. Cosmas of Maiuma.

[^1631]: Moses stretching out his hands to support Israel against Amalek (Exodus 17:11–12) is the primary Old Testament type of the Cross.

[^1632]: The bronze serpent lifted on a pole by Moses in the wilderness (Numbers 21:8–9; John 3:14).

[^1633]: Commemorating the appearance of the Cross in the sky to the Emperor Constantine before the Battle of the Milvian Bridge.

[^1634]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer with acrostic.

[^1635]: Troparion of St. Eumenius, petitioning his intercession before Christ.

[^1636]: Continuation of Troparion of Ode 1 for St. Eumenius, overcoming the serpent and receiving gifts of healing.

[^1637]: Fasting and the subjugation of the fleshly mind unto the spirit (cf. Romans 8:5–7).

[^1638]: The mystery of the Incarnation of the Word in two perfect natures (divine and human), fulfilling the prophecies.

[^1639]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, citing the blossoming of Aaron's rod (Numbers 17:8) and the fruitfulness of the barren Church (Isaiah 54:1).

[^1640]: Moses striking the rock in the wilderness (Exodus 17:6; Numbers 20:11; 1 Corinthians 10:4) as a type of Christ and the Church.

[^1641]: The piercing of Christ's side (John 19:34; 1 John 5:6–8) as the source of baptism and the Eucharist.

[^1642]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2.

[^1643]: The patristic metaphor of the virtuous hierarch as a spiritual bee gathering the honey of virtues (cf. St. Basil the Great, *Address to Young Men*).

[^1644]: Speech seasoned with salt, reflecting Colossians 4:6.

[^1645]: Alluding to 2 Corinthians 2:15: "For we are unto God a sweet savour of Christ."

[^1646]: The Mother of God invoked as the golden censer (Hebrews 9:4), purifying from the spiritual stench of passions.

[^1647]: Kontakion of St. Eumenius, Tone 2.

[^1648]: St. Eumenius, wonderworking bishop of Gortyna in Crete.

[^1649]: Sessional Hymn of the Afterfeast of the Exaltation, Tone 5.

[^1650]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, citing Habakkuk 3:2.

[^1651]: The sweetening of the waters of Marah (Exodus 15:23–25) prefiguring the calling of the Gentiles.

[^1652]: The iron axe-head made buoyant by the stick cast by Elisha (2 Kings [4 Kingdoms] 6:5–7).

[^1653]: The cruciform encampment of the four divisions of Israel around the Tabernacle (Numbers 2).

[^1654]: Citing Psalm 18:2 [LXX Ps. 18:1]: "The heavens declare the glory of God."

[^1655]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2, echoing Habakkuk 3:2.

[^1656]: The saint's contemplation and liturgical life in the temples of God.

[^1657]: St. Eumenius served as Bishop of Gortyna in Crete during the 7th century.

[^1658]: St. Eumenius was renowned for slaying a monstrous venomous serpent through prayer.

[^1659]: The Old Testament prophecies foretelling the virgin birth of Christ.

[^1660]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8.

[^1661]: The flaming turning sword guarding Eden (Genesis 3:24) yielding before the Cross.

[^1662]: Philippians 2:10: "That at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth."

[^1663]: The illumination of the Gentile nations through the preaching of the Cross.

[^1664]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2, citing Isaiah 26:9, 13.

[^1665]: St. Eumenius was celebrated as a peacemaker between rulers and civil authorities.

[^1666]: Historical accounts record St. Eumenius reconciling imperial rulers and resolving civic strife.

[^1667]: Later in life, St. Eumenius traveled to Rome where he worked miracles, and later died in exile in the Thebaid.

[^1668]: An explicit Dyothelite theological formula: Christ possesses two natural wills and operations (divine and human), affirming the definition of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681).

[^1669]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, evoking Jonah as a type of Christ's three-day burial and resurrection (Jonah 2; Matthew 12:40).

[^1670]: Jacob crossing his hands to bless Ephraim and Manasseh (Genesis 48:14), canceling the handwriting of ordinances (Colossians 2:14).

[^1671]: Jacob blessing Joseph's sons Ephraim and Manasseh with crossed hands (Genesis 48:14, 19).

[^1672]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2, invoking Jonah 2:2–3.

[^1673]: St. Eumenius distributed imperial gifts to the poor, counting gold as dirt.

[^1674]: St. Eumenius's episcopal peacemaking and pastoral preaching.

[^1675]: The ascetic crossing the tempestuous sea of passions without spiritual harm.

[^1676]: The Incarnation of the Word portrayed as the heavenly dew descending upon the fleece (Judges 6:36–40; Psalm 71:6 [LXX Ps. 71:6]).

[^1677]: The Pochaiv text prints "благовернаго королѧ нашего" (our faithful King), referring to King Augustus III of Poland, with a blank/erased space for the sovereign's name characteristic of the 18th-century Pochaiv editions under Polish-Lithuanian rule.

[^1678]: Festal Kontakion of the Exaltation of the Precious Cross (*Вознесы́йсѧ на крⷭ҇тъ во́лею*), Tone 4.

[^1679]: St. Paul's third-heaven revelation (2 Corinthians 12:2–4) and boast in the Cross of Christ (Galatians 6:14).

[^1680]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, citing Daniel 3.

[^1681]: The ancestral fall of Adam through the forbidden fruit and bodily mortality (Genesis 3:6, 19; Romans 5:12).

[^1682]: The Tree of Life barred after Adam's transgression (Genesis 3:24) and reopened to the repentant thief on the cross (Luke 23:42–43).

[^1683]: Israel blessing the top of Joseph's staff (Genesis 47:31; Hebrews 11:21) prefiguring the victory of the Cross.

[^1684]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2, reflecting Daniel 3.

[^1685]: In his extreme old age, St. Eumenius undertook arduous pastoral travels to strengthen the churches.

[^1686]: St. Eumenius was exiled from Rome to the Egyptian Thebaid, where he ended a prolonged drought by his intercessions.

[^1687]: St. Eumenius's ascetic growth from spiritual infancy to mature virtue (Ephesians 4:13).

[^1688]: Citing Luke 1:42: "Blessed is the fruit of thy womb."

[^1689]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8, reflecting the Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:57ff.).

[^1690]: The cosmic exaltation of the Cross sprinkled with the Savior's blood.

[^1691]: The holy lance piercing Christ's side (John 19:34) venerated alongside the Cross.

[^1692]: The Cross as the divine standard granting victory unto Christian sovereigns over barbarian onslaughts.

[^1693]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2.

[^1694]: St. Eumenius finished his course in the Egyptian Thebaid (2 Timothy 4:7; Job 5:26).

[^1695]: The translation of St. Eumenius's holy relics back to his native Crete from the Thebaid.

[^1696]: St. Eumenius's sacred relics were entombed alongside those of his predecessor, St. Cyril, Bishop of Gortyna.

[^1697]: Ezekiel 44:2: the shut gate through which the Lord alone passes, prefiguring the perpetual virginity of the Mother of God.

[^1698]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 8.

[^1699]: Troparion of Ode 9 of the Feast, citing the sweetening of Marah (Exodus 15:23–25).

[^1700]: The Cross restoring mankind from the ancestral fall of Adam.

[^1701]: The celestial appearance of the Cross unto Emperor Constantine with the inscription *In this sign conquer*.

[^1702]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Eumenius, Tone 2.

[^1703]: St. Eumenius ascending from virtue to virtue (Psalm 83:8 [LXX Ps. 83:7]).

[^1704]: Petitioning St. Eumenius's intercession against foreign barbarian invasions and domestic perils.

[^1705]: The liturgical commemoration of St. Eumenius assembling clergy and faithful.

[^1706]: The Mother of God nursing Him Who sustains the cosmos (Hebrews 1:3; Luke 1:48).

[^1707]: Festal Exapostilarion of the Exaltation of the Cross.

[^1708]: Aposticha Sticheron 1 of the Afterfeast, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha."

[^1709]: Aposticha Sticheron 2 of the Afterfeast, summoning monastics to venerate the Cross.

[^1712]: Liturgical rubric for the Divine Liturgy during the Afterfeast of the Exaltation.

[^1716]: Sticheron 3 at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 4, praying for peace in the Church and victory over foreign adversaries.

[^1717]: Sticheron 1 of the Holy Martyrs, Tone 8, dedicated to St. Trophimus.

[^1719]: Sticheron 3 of the Holy Martyrs, Tone 8, dedicated to St. Dorymedon, a senator who openly confessed Christ and was martyred.

[^1720]: Festal Doxasticon at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 4, recalling Christ's Passion, the darkening of the sun, and the tearing of the veil (Matthew 27:45, 51).

[^1842]: Troparion 3 of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Saint, praising his perseverance through life's trials.

[^1843]: Theotokion of Ode 3 of the Canon of the Saint, praising the Mother of God as the pure dwelling of virginity.

[^1844]: Festal Kontakion of the Exaltation of the Cross, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross." In this Pochaiv print, the text commemorates the Polish-Lithuanian monarch: "our orthodox king" (бл҃говѣ́рнаго королѧ̀ на́шего).

[^1845]: Festal Ikos of the Exaltation of the Cross, quoting the Apostle Paul caught up to the third heaven (2 Corinthians 12:2–4) and his epistle to the Galatians (Galatians 6:14).

[^1846]: Matins Sessional Hymn of St. Eustathius after Ode 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom," recounting his vision of the Cross upon the stag and quoting Job 1:21.

[^1847]: Festal Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 8, concluding from leaf p264, recounting Empress Helen's exhortation to Emperor Constantine.

[^1848]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, citing Habakkuk 3:2.

[^1849]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Exaltation, recalling Moses sweetening the waters of Marah (Exodus 15:23–25).

[^1850]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Exaltation, citing the Prophet Elisha retrieving the sunken iron axe-head by casting wood into the Jordan (2 Kings 6:5–7; LXX 4 Kings 6:5–7).

[^1851]: Troparion 3 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Exaltation, describing the cruciform encampment of the twelve tribes around the Tabernacle (Numbers 2:1–34).

[^1852]: Troparion 4 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Exaltation, citing Psalm 18:1 (LXX Ps. 18:1).

[^1853]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, citing Habakkuk 3:11 on the sun standing in its course.

[^1854]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Saint, commemorating St. Theopiste preserved in chastity during her captivity.

[^1855]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Saint, praising St. Eustathius' fortitude in the arena of martyrdom.

[^1856]: Troparion 3 of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Saint, commemorating St. Eustathius, his wife, and children.

[^1857]: Theotokion of Ode 4 of the Canon of the Saint, addressing the Ever-Virgin Theotokos.

[^1858]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, praising the thrice-blessed Tree of the Cross.

[^1859]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Exaltation, concluding from leaf p265, referencing the cherubim guarding Eden with a flaming sword (Genesis 3:24).

[^1860]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Exaltation, citing Philippians 2:10 on all knees bowing to Christ.

[^1861]: Troparion 3 of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Exaltation, celebrating the illumination of the Gentiles by the Cross.

[^1862]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, citing John 12:46 on Christ coming as a light into the world.

[^1863]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Saint, comparing St. Eustathius' blamelessness and patience to Job (Job 1:1).

[^1864]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Saint, lamenting his severe sufferings in being separated from his family.

[^1865]: Theotokion of Ode 5 of the Canon of the Saint, invoking the Virgin Mary to heal the soul's affliction.

[^1866]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, citing Jonah in the belly of the whale (Jonah 2:1–10) as a prefiguration of Christ's Cross and three-day burial.

[^1867]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Exaltation, referencing Jacob crossing his hands to bless Ephraim and Manasseh (Genesis 48:13–14).

[^1868]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Exaltation, completing the typological interpretation of Jacob blessing the younger son above the elder (Genesis 48:14–19).

[^1869]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, citing Jonah 2:9 and John 19:34.

[^1870]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Saint, praising St. Eustathius adorned with the royal purple of his blood.

[^1871]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Saint, celebrating his partaking in the Passions and Kingdom of Christ (Romans 8:17).

[^1872]: Theotokion of Ode 6 of the Canon of the Saint, quoting Gabriel's greeting (Luke 1:28) and Malachi 4:2 on the Sun of righteousness.

[^1873]: Kontakion of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest," citing Matthew 20:22 on drinking Christ's cup and Romans 8:17 on being fellow-heirs of His glory.

[^1874]: Ikos of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, invoking Christ to inspire praise for the holy passion-bearer.

[^1875]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, referencing the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:1–28).

[^1876]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Exaltation, contrasting the tasting of the tree in Genesis 3 with salvation through the Tree of the Cross.

[^1877]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Exaltation, recalling the closing of the Tree of Life (Genesis 3:22–24) and its opening unto the Good Thief (Luke 23:42–43).

[^1878]: Troparion 3 of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Exaltation, concluding from leaf p267, citing Jacob leaning upon the top of Joseph's staff (Genesis 47:31; Hebrews 11:21).

[^1879]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, quoting Daniel 3:52.

[^1880]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Saint, commemorating the martyrdom of St. Eustathius, St. Theopiste, and their sons inside the red-hot brazen bull under Emperor Hadrian.

[^1881]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Saint, praising their united endurance in martyrdom.

[^1882]: Theotokion of Ode 7 of the Canon of the Saint, addressing the Theotokos higher than the heavenly powers.

[^1883]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, praising the Holy Trinity and the Holy Spirit Who gives life unto all.

[^1884]: Troparion 1 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Exaltation, celebrating the recall of the earthborn through the Cross of Christ.

[^1885]: Troparion 2 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Exaltation, recalling the piercing of Christ's side with the lance (John 19:34; Luke 3:6; Isaiah 52:10).

[^1886]: Troparion 3 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Exaltation, citing Psalm 67:31 (LXX Ps. 67:31) on scattering the nations that delight in war.

[^1887]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, citing Daniel in the lions' den (Daniel 6:16–22) and the Three Children in the furnace (Daniel 3:19–27; Song of the Three Children 1:35).

[^1888]: Troparion 1 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Saint, praising the four martyrs as a harmonious choir of true witnesses.

[^1889]: Troparion 2 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Saint, recalling their reunion after years of separation and their martyrdom together.

[^1890]: Troparion 3 of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Saint, praising St. Eustathius offering his family unto Christ as a holy oblation.

[^1891]: Theotokion of Ode 8 of the Canon of the Saint, citing the prophecy of the rod from the root of Jesse (Isaiah 11:1).

[^1892]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of the Exaltation, Tone 8, venerating the Theotokos through whom the ancestral curse of Genesis 3 was undone.

[^1893]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Exaltation, citing the sweetening of the waters of Marah (Exodus 15:23–25) as a type of the Cross.

[^1894]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Exaltation, celebrating the restoration of fallen human nature by the light of the Cross.

[^1895]: Troparion 3 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Exaltation, commemorating the vision of the radiant Cross in the sky granted to Emperor Constantine.

[^1896]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius, Tone 4, citing Daniel 2:34, 45 on the stone cut out of the mountain without hands.

[^1897]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Saint, concluding from leaf p269, invoking St. Eustathius' intercession before the Trinity.

[^1898]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Saint, asking for a share in the heavenly joy granted to the righteous.

[^1899]: Troparion 3 of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Saint, personal prayer of the hymnographer seeking St. Eustathius' patronage.

[^1900]: Theotokion of Ode 9 of the Canon of the Saint, asking for deliverance from all sorrow through the prayers of the Mother of God.

[^1901]: Festal Exapostilarion (Photogogicon) of the Holy Great-martyr Eustathius and his family, Tone 3 (or Tone 2), Podoben: "In spirit in the sanctuary."

[^1902]: Festal Theotokion / Exapostilarion of the Cross, Tone 3 (or Tone 2), hailing the Cross as armor and weapons against Belial.

[^1903]: Sticheron 1 at the Praises, Tone 4, Automelon, composed by St. Andrew of Pyrgos (or Andrew of Crete / Andrew the Blind), recounting St. Eustathius' patience and quoting Job 1:21.

[^1904]: Sticheron 2 at the Praises, Tone 6, praising St. Eustathius as an abode of the uncreated Trinity.

[^1905]: Sticheron 3 at the Praises, Tone 8, recounting St. Eustathius' vision of Christ upon the stag and his illumination through baptism (John 3:5).

[^1906]: *Glory, Tone 8. By Germanus:* Attributed to Germanus I, Patriarch of Constantinople (ca. 658–740). The hymn references the vision of Christ in the form of a stag seen by the Roman general Placidus prior to his baptism as Eustathius.

[^1907]: The hymn weaves together the typology of Moses holding his hands in the form of a cross to conquer Amalek (Ex. 17:8–13), David's injunction to worship the footstool of the Lord (Ps. 98:5 LXX), and the confession of the wise thief upon Golgotha (Luke 23:42–43).

[^1908]: Podoben *O House of Ephratha* (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ).

[^1909]: Psalm 98:5 LXX.

[^1910]: Psalm 73:12 LXX.

[^1911]: Alluding to Gal. 1:1 ('not of men, neither by man, but by Jesus Christ') and Heb. 12:4 ('Ye have not yet resisted unto blood, striving against sin').

[^1912]: Allusion to the prophetic witness of Deut. 28:66 LXX ('And thy life shall be hanging before thine eyes'), understood by the Church Fathers as a prophecy of the Crucifixion; and to Ps. 150:5 ('praise Him on the high-sounding cymbals') and Ps. 73:12 LXX.

[^1913]: Concluding doxological formula of the Aposticha Doxasticon of the Feast, completing the sentence begun at the foot of fol. 135r (leaf p271).

[^1914]: Scriptural citations for the Liturgy of the Great Martyr Eustathius: Prokeimenon, Ps. 15:3, 8 LXX; Epistle, Eph. 6:10–17 (pericope 233); Alleluia, Ps. 33:18 LXX; Gospel, Luke 21:12–19 (pericope 106); Communion Verse, Ps. 32:1 LXX.

[^1915]: September 21: Commemoration of the holy Apostle Quadratus of the Seventy, Bishop of Athens and Magnesia (d. ca. 130). In the Ruthenian Typikon, because the Apodosis of the Exaltation of the Holy Cross falls on September 21, the Apostle's office is transferred to September 22 to be chanted together with the Hieromartyr Phocas of Sinope.

[^1916]: *Leavetaking of the Exaltation of the Precious and Life-giving Cross* (Ѿда́нїе воздви́женїѧ честна́гѡ крⷭ҇та̀). On this day, the entire service of the Feast of the Exaltation (September 14) is repeated without change, except for the entrance at Vespers and the polyeleos at Matins.

[^1917]: Rubrics governing the occurrence of the Apodosis on a Saturday, specifying the chanting of the Resurrection Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone at the entrance of Vespers.

[^1918]: Heirmoi of Ode 9 of the two festal canons of the Exaltation of the Cross: the first canon composed by St. Cosmas the Melodist (*Та́инъ є҆сѝ бцⷣе ра́й*), and the second canon by St. John Damascene (*Снѣ́дїю дре́ва*).

[^1919]: At the Liturgy on the Apodosis, the ninth ode of the festal canon is appointed for the Beatitudes on 8; the ordinary readings of the day (epistle and gospel) are read.

[^1920]: The solemn liturgical ceremony of the return of the Precious Cross to the sanctuary: after being exposed in the center of the nave upon the tetrapod since September 14, the Cross is venerated, censed, and carried back into the altar through the Royal Doors and placed upon the Holy Table (Antimension).

[^1921]: Typikon chapter detailing the liturgical synthesis when the Apodosis of the Exaltation coincides with a Sunday, combining the Resurrectional Oktoechos with the Festal Menaion.

[^1922]: Alternating performance of the Sunday Kontakion and the Festal Kontakion at the Little Hours when the Apodosis coincides with a Sunday.

[^1923]: Liturgical pericope apparatus for the Sunday after the Exaltation when combined with the Apodosis.

[^1924]: Rubrical note reiterating the transfer of the commemoration of the holy Apostle Quadratus to September 22.

[^1925]: The seasonal winter change in the Psalter cursus: after the Apodosis of the Exaltation of the Holy Cross (from September 22), the daily Matins kathisma allocation increases from two to three kathismata, and Kathisma 18 (the Songs of Ascents, Pss. 119–133 LXX) is appointed for daily Vespers.

[^1926]: Typikon rule governing the polyeleos on all Sundays from the autumn equinox until December 20 (the Forefeast of the Nativity).

[^1927]: Prescriptions for the Katavasia: beginning on September 22, the Heirmoi of the Annunciation Canon (*Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆́ста моѧ̑* / *I shall open my mouth*) are chanted until the Forefeast of the Entry of the Theotokos (November 20/21).

[^1928]: September 22 commemorates: (1) St. Phocas, Hieromartyr and Bishop of Sinope (d. 117); (2) the holy Prophet Jonah (8th c. BC); (3) St. Jonah the Presbyter (9th c.), father of the iconodules St. Theophanes the Hymnographer and St. Theodore the Branded (*Graptoi*); and (4) St. Quadratus of the Seventy (transferred from Sept. 21).

[^1929]: Allusion to 1 Pet. 5:1 ('a witness of the sufferings of Christ, and also a partaker of the glory that shall be revealed').

[^1930]: Podoben *Of the heavenly ranks* (Небⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ).

[^1931]: St. Phocas of Sinope was celebrated throughout the Black Sea and Mediterranean as the patron saint and miraculous protector of sailors and navigators.

[^1932]: Doxasticon of St. Phocas at "Lord, I have cried," attributed to Cyprian (likely Cyprian the Studite or Cyprian of Antioch).

[^1933]: Stavrotheotokion in Tone 4, alluding to Job 26:7 ('He hangs the earth upon nothing').

[^1934]: Common Troparion of an Apostle in Tone 3.

[^1935]: Common Troparion of a Hieromartyr (*И нравом причастник*), alluding to 2 Tim. 2:15 ('rightly dividing the word of truth').

[^1936]: The joint canon for the Prophet Jonah and the Venerable Jonah the Presbyter is transferred to Compline because the Apostle Quadratus and Hieromartyr Phocas occupy the primary offices of Vespers and Matins.

[^1937]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 2, celebrating the overthrow of Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Ex. 15:1–19).

[^1938]: Allusion to the mission of the Prophet Jonah to Nineveh (Jonah 3:1–10).

[^1939]: St. Jonah the Presbyter embraced monastic life at the Monastery of St. Sabbas the Sanctified in Palestine, together with his two sons, Theophanes and Theodore.

[^1940]: Christ as the Sun of Righteousness (Mal. 4:2), invisible in His divinity before the ages, yet circumscribed according to His human nature in the Incarnation.

[^1941]: Ode 3 Heirmos, based on the Prayer of Hannah (1 Sam. 2:1–2 LXX / 1 Kings 2:1–2).

[^1942]: Jonah's reluctance to preach to Nineveh stemmed patristically from his prophetic foreknowledge that the Gentiles would accept repentance while Israel would reject it, provoking his sorrow.

[^1943]: Jonah 1:8–15: the prophet confesses his Hebrew heritage and faith in the God of heaven to the mariners before being cast into the sea.

[^1944]: St. Jonah consecrated his two sons, Theophanes and Theodore, to the service of Christ and the monastic life in the Lavra of St. Sabbas.

[^1945]: Ode 4 Heirmos, based on the Prayer of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2–19 LXX).

[^1946]: Jonah 1:17 (2:1 LXX) and Matt. 12:40: the three days and three nights in the belly of the whale as a type of the three-day burial and Resurrection of Christ.

[^1947]: Jonah 4:2: Jonah explains that he fled because he knew God to be gracious, merciful, slow to anger, and repentant of evil.

[^1948]: Commending St. Jonah's hospitality (Gen. 18:1–8) and his transition from the married priesthood to the monastic life with his sons.

[^1949]: Ode 5 Heirmos, based on the Prayer of Isaiah (Isa. 26:9–20).

[^1950]: Typological fulfillment of Jonah's three days in Christ's entombed body in the stone sepulchre (Matt. 12:40; 27:60).

[^1951]: The repentance of the Ninevites at the preaching of Jonah (Jonah 3:5–10).

[^1952]: 2 Tim. 4:7–8: 'I have kept the faith: henceforth there is laid up for me a crown of righteousness.'

[^1953]: Chalcedonian Christological confession of the one Hypostasis in two essences (natures) born of the Virgin Mary.

[^1954]: Ode 6 Heirmos, based on the Prayer of Jonah from the belly of the fish (Jonah 2:1–9 LXX).

[^1955]: Deliverance from the spiritual sea monster (the devil/serpent) patterned after Jonah's miraculous preservation.

[^1956]: Jonah 3:4: Jonah cries out: 'Yet forty days, and Nineveh shall be overthrown!'

[^1957]: St. Jonah's pastoral care as presbyter and hegumen/almoner in the Palestinian monastic wilderness.

[^1958]: Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben *Speedily anticipate* (Ско́рѡ предварѝ), drawing directly on Jonah 2:6 (2:7 LXX): 'Let my life ascend from corruption, O Lord my God.'

[^1959]: Ode 7 Heirmos, based on the Prayer of the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian furnace (Dan. 3:26–45).

[^1960]: Jonah 4:6: the Lord God appointed a gourd (plant/vine) to shadow Jonah's head from the fierce sun.

[^1961]: Patristic tradition identifying Jonah as the son of the widow of Zarephath, raised from the dead by the Prophet Elijah (1 Kings 17:17–24).

[^1962]: St. Jonah's entrance into the heavenly sanctuary (*the highest temple*) following his earthly priestly liturgies.

[^1963]: The Virgin Mary as the radiant cloud carrying Christ the Sun of Righteousness (cf. Isa. 19:1).

[^1964]: Ode 8 Heirmos, based on the Song of the Three Holy Youths (Dan. 3:52–88).

[^1965]: Jonah's outstretched hands in the whale's belly forming a cross, prefiguring Christ's Crucifixion and Resurrection (Jonah 2:1–9).

[^1966]: Ode 9 Heirmos, based on the Magnificat and the Benedictus (Luke 1:46–55, 68–79).

[^1967]: The sign of the Prophet Jonah as the supreme typological pledge of Christ's third-day Resurrection (Matt. 12:39–40; Luke 11:29–30). The hymnography of Ode 9 continues across the leaf boundary onto fol. 140v (leaf p281).

[^1968]: Conclusion of the final Troparion of the Prophet Jonah from Ode 9 of his canon, bridging from fol. 140r (leaf p280) onto fol. 140v (leaf p281).

[^1969]: Venerable Jonah the Presbyter, father of Ss. Theophanes the Hymnographer and Theodore the Branded (Graptoi), celebrated for his Orthodox defense of the Trinity and sacred icons.

[^1970]: The heavenly dwelling place of St. Jonah, who ministered as presbyter and monastic elder in Palestine.

[^1971]: First sticheron of the Prophet Jonah at the Aposticha/Praises, Tone 4, Podoben *Thou hast given a sign* (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе), recalling Jonah's typological prophecy of Christ's three-day Resurrection.

[^1972]: Jonah's flight to Tarshish (Jonah 1:1–3), prompted by his foreknowledge that God's mercy would spare the Gentiles of Nineveh.

[^1973]: Jonah 3:4 and 4:1–2: Jonah's sorrow when God showed mercy upon the repentant Ninevites.

[^1974]: Theotokion petitioning for a merciful defense and placement at the right hand of Christ at the Dread Judgment (cf. Matt. 25:31–34).

[^1975]: Conclusion of the Wednesday/Friday Stavrotheotokion, bridging from fol. 140v (leaf p281) onto fol. 141r (leaf p282).

[^1976]: Canon of the holy Apostle Quadratus of the Seventy, Bishop of Athens and Magnesia, composed in Tone 2 with an acrostic.

[^1977]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Apostle, referencing the Exodus through the Red Sea (Exod. 14:21–31).

[^1978]: St. Quadratus presenting his apostolic crown before the King of glory.

[^1979]: Quadratus's famous Apology for the Christian faith, presented to Emperor Hadrian, which silenced pagan deception.

[^1980]: St. Quadratus's priestly proclamation of the Gospel.

[^1981]: Dogmatic affirmation of the perpetual virginity of the Theotokos before, during, and after childbearing.

[^1982]: Canon of the holy Hieromartyr Phocas, Bishop of Sinope, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer in Tone 4.

[^1983]: Ode 1 Heirmos of the Canon of the Hieromartyr, drawing on Israel's victory over Amalek through Moses' outstretched arms (Exod. 17:11–12).

[^1984]: Invocation of St. Phocas's pastoral and martyric intercession.

[^1985]: St. Phocas's episcopal robes dyed in his own martyric blood.

[^1986]: The martyr's victory over the demonic adversary through heavenly hope.

[^1987]: The Incarnation looses the primal ancestral curse of Adam and Eve.

[^1988]: Ode 3 Heirmos conclusion of the Canon of the Apostle, bridging from fol. 141r (leaf p282) onto fol. 141v (leaf p283).

[^1989]: Miracles wrought by Apostle Quadratus confirming the preaching of the Gospel.

[^1990]: Quadratus's illumination of Greece and Asia Minor with apostolic doctrine.

[^1991]: Pastoral care and protection of the flock against idolatrous ignorance.

[^1992]: The divine fire of the Godhead enclosed within the Virgin's womb without burning her (cf. the Burning Bush, Exod. 3:2).

[^1993]: Ode 3 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4.

[^1994]: St. Phocas's healing miracles delivering Sinope from pestilence and demonic delusions.

[^1995]: The overthrow of pagan temples and idols in Sinope by the prayers of St. Phocas.

[^1996]: Angelic radiance and heavenly glory manifested during the trial and tortures of St. Phocas.

[^1997]: Christ's condescension in taking human nature to destroy the ancient condemnation.

[^1998]: Kontakion of St. Phocas, Tone 6, modeled on the festal kontakion *Fulfilling the dispensation* (Є҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ).

[^1999]: St. Phocas offering himself as a living sacrifice in imitation of the Chief High Priest Christ.

[^2000]: Conclusion of the Ikos of Hieromartyr Phocas, bridging from fol. 141v (leaf p283) onto fol. 142r (leaf p284).

[^2001]: Sessional hymn of the Apostle Quadratus, Tone 3, Podoben *Awed by the beauty* (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства).

[^2002]: Quadratus's apostolic mission as hierarch of Athens and Magnesia, illuminating the gentiles with the light of Christ.

[^2003]: Sessional hymn of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, Podoben *Thy tomb* (Гро́бъ тво́й).

[^2004]: St. Phocas's priestly vesture consecrated and beautified by his martyric blood.

[^2005]: Theotokion petitioning for spiritual amendment of life and remission of sins through the intercession of the Virgin Mary.

[^2006]: Stavrotheotokion lamenting the Crucified Savior, sung on Wednesday or Friday.

[^2007]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of Apostle Quadratus, based on the prophetic prayer of Habakkuk (Hab. 3:2 LXX).

[^2008]: Metaphor of apostolic asceticism: cultivating the heart and uprooting vices with the sickle of divine words.

[^2009]: Healings granted at the relics and sepulchre of Apostle Quadratus.

[^2010]: Supplication for St. Quadratus's swift aid against perils and tribulations.

[^2011]: St. Quadratus burning up pagan idolatry and philosophical falsehoods like combustible thorns.

[^2012]: The Word of God taking flesh from the all-pure blood of the Virgin Mary to renew fallen human nature.

[^2013]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on Habakkuk 3:11.

[^2014]: Conclusion of the first troparion of Ode 4 for St. Phocas, bridging from fol. 142r (leaf p284) onto fol. 142v (leaf p285).

[^2015]: St. Phocas's courageous confession before the pagan magistrates of Sinope during Trajan's persecution.

[^2016]: St. Phocas as the celestial patron and haven of mariners and seafarers across the Black Sea and Mediterranean.

[^2017]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of the Apostle, based on Isaiah 26:9.

[^2018]: Christ purifying defiled souls through the apostolic preaching and intercessions of St. Quadratus.

[^2019]: The Athenian philosophers and rhetoricians yielding to the apostolic apologetics and dogmatic teachings of Quadratus.

[^2020]: Quadratus proclaiming the uncreated light from the Father of lights (James 1:17) and the Holy Trinity.

[^2021]: The orthodox confession of Christ existing in two natures, begotten of the Father before the ages and born of the Virgin in time.

[^2022]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on John 8:12 and 12:46.

[^2023]: St. Phocas treading the steep path of martyrdom according to the divine will.

[^2024]: The bodily tortures of St. Phocas breaking the spiritual fetters of demonic paganism.

[^2025]: The martyr's self-oblation mirroring Christ's sacrifice and abolishing the blood sacrifices of idols.

[^2026]: Conclusion of the Ode 5 Theotokion of St. Phocas, bridging from fol. 142v (leaf p285) onto fol. 143r (leaf p286); cf. Proverbs 9:1.

[^2027]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of the Apostle, based on Jonah 2:2.

[^2028]: Apostle Quadratus as a mystical heaven proclaiming the glory of God (Ps. 18:2 LXX).

[^2029]: Defense of the unity and unoriginate nature of the Holy Trinity against polytheistic Hellenism.

[^2030]: Incorrupt wonders and miracles performed through the relics of St. Quadratus.

[^2031]: The Mother of God revealed as a living, earthly heaven through whom divine light illumined humanity.

[^2032]: Ode 6 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on Jonah 2:9.

[^2033]: St. Phocas quenching the sea of heathenism and pouring out spiritual healings upon believers.

[^2034]: St. Phocas suspended on the torture post, sustained by a heavenly voice during his martyrdom.

[^2035]: St. Phocas, having suffered impassibly with Christ, delivering voyagers from stormy shipwrecks.

[^2036]: The Word of God descending into the Virgin's womb like dew and rain upon the fleece (Ps. 71:6 LXX).

[^2037]: Kontakion of Apostle Quadratus, Tone 8, Podoben *As first-fruits* (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки).

[^2038]: The universal Church offering Apostle Quadratus as an intercessor for the pardon of sins.

[^2039]: Conclusion of the Ikos of Apostle Quadratus, bridging from fol. 143r (leaf p286) onto fol. 143v (leaf p287).

[^2040]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of the Apostle, based on Daniel 3:1–23.

[^2041]: Apostle Quadratus elevating his spiritual flock above demonic affliction through theology.

[^2042]: Demolition of pagan temples through Quadratus's prayers and erection of Christian sanctuaries.

[^2043]: St. Quadratus filled with the Holy Spirit as a flame consuming the dry brushwood of pagan deception.

[^2044]: Theotokion honoring the Mother of God through whom the curse of Eve was repealed.

[^2045]: Ode 7 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on the Song of the Three Children.

[^2046]: St. Phocas willingly offering himself like a spotless lamb to Christ the Good Shepherd.

[^2047]: St. Phocas cast into a boiling caldron / scalding bath during his martyrdom in Sinope, miraculously unharmed like gold refined in a furnace.

[^2048]: Conclusion of the third troparion of Ode 7 for St. Phocas, bridging from fol. 143v (leaf p287) onto fol. 144r (leaf p288).

[^2049]: Supplication to the Theotokos for the renewal and restoration of a soul battered by sin.

[^2050]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of the Apostle, based on the Song of the Three Holy Youths (Dan. 3:52–88).

[^2051]: Quadratus standing firmly upon Christ, the spiritual Rock (cf. 1 Cor. 10:4).

[^2052]: St. Quadratus delivering souls from idolatrous frenzies through evangelical truth.

[^2053]: Apostle Quadratus shining like lightning from Christ the Sun of Righteousness.

[^2054]: The ancient Old Testament prophecies prefiguring the Divine Maternity of the Virgin Mary.

[^2055]: Ode 8 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on Daniel 6:22 and Daniel 3.

[^2056]: St. Phocas's pastoral eloquence reclaiming sinners ensnared by the primordial serpent (Satan); cf. Psalm 44:3 (LXX).

[^2057]: Invocation of St. Phocas's patronage over turbulent seas and maritime distress.

[^2058]: The shrine and church of St. Phocas in Sinope as a haven of physical and spiritual healing.

[^2059]: Conclusion of the final troparion of Ode 8 for St. Phocas, bridging from fol. 144r (leaf p288) onto fol. 144v (leaf p289).

[^2060]: Old Testament tabernacle typology applied to the Mother of God (Exod. 25:31–40; Heb. 9:1–5).

[^2061]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of Apostle Quadratus, praising the Incarnation of God the Word.

[^2062]: Apostle Quadratus interceding for universal peace before the throne of Christ.

[^2063]: The precious relics of St. Quadratus preserved in Magnesia as an ark of divine grace.

[^2064]: Commemoration of St. Quadratus celebrated with faith and prayer for forgiveness.

[^2065]: Eschatological prayer to Christ through the intercessions of the Theotokos, apostles, prophets, and martyrs (cf. Ps. 6:2 LXX).

[^2066]: Ode 9 Heirmos of the Canon of Hieromartyr Phocas, Tone 4, based on Daniel 2:34, 45 (the uncut stone cut out from the mountain).

[^2067]: Liturgical invitation to praise the memory of St. Phocas the bishop and shepherd.

[^2068]: The martyrdom of St. Phocas in the overheated bath, yielding his soul peacefully into the hands of God.

[^2069]: Conclusion of the third troparion of Ode 9 for St. Phocas, bridging from fol. 144v (leaf p289) onto fol. 145r (leaf p290).

[^2070]: Universal veneration of St. Phocas's martyric triumph across the Christian world.

[^2071]: Supplication to the Theotokos for deliverance from eternal punishment and illumination of soul.

[^2072]: Exapostilarion of Apostle Quadratus, Podoben *In the spirit in the sanctuary* (Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лище).

[^2073]: The apostle's ascent into the heavenly sanctuary, beholding the Holy Trinity; cf. Romans 10:15; Isaiah 52:7.

[^2074]: Exapostilarion Glory of Hieromartyr Phocas, Podoben *The sky with stars* (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми).

[^2075]: St. Phocas venerated as the ornament of hierarchs and defender of the poor and indigent.

[^2076]: Marian petition invoking the maternal bold intercession of the Virgin Mary before Christ.

[^2077]: Liturgy pericope appointments for the commemoration of the Hieromartyr Phocas and Apostle Quadratus on September 22: Epistle Heb. 13:7–16 (pericope 334); Gospel John 10:9–16 (pericope 36).

[^2079]: The customary Kathisma on weekday evenings outside festal seasons is Kathisma 18 (Psalms 119–133).

[^2086]: The printed Pochaiv recension reads *бы́вшїй* (*byvshii*, "who hast been [above all the prophets]"), where standard synodal editions read *бо́льшїй* (*bol'shii*, "O greatest of all the prophets", cf. Matt. 11:11).

[^2107]: The Holy Protomartyr and Equal-to-the-Apostles Thekla of Iconium (1st century), disciple of the Apostle Paul.

[^2108]: According to the ancient *Acts of Paul and Thekla*, Thekla sat at her window for three days listening to the preaching of the Apostle Paul in the neighboring house of Onesiphorus.

[^2109]: Reference to the Apostle Paul being driven out or imprisoned by the magistrates of Iconium.

[^2110]: Slavonic *ла́пꙋ* (*lapu*), representing the pyre or stake to which she was led in the arena.

[^2111]: Slavonic *дла́мꙋра* (*dlamura*), a scribal/phonetic variant in Ukrainian/Ruthenian typography for Thamyris (Θάμυρις), the wealthy pagan suitor to whom Thekla was betrothed before hearing St. Paul.

[^2112]: Luke 2:35.

[^2113]: Stavrotheotokion of Tone 8.

[^2114]: The mention of Peter alongside Paul reflects the composite Ruthenian-Byzantine liturgical tradition honoring the Holy Apostles Peter and Paul as joint pillars of apostolic faith.

[^2115]: Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8, composed by St. John the Monk (John Damascene).

[^2116]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8 (referencing Exod. 15:1–19).

[^2117]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8.

[^2118]: An allusion to the *Acts of Paul and Thekla*, wherein Thekla's mother, Theocleia, sought in vain to dissuade her daughter from embracing Christian virginity and following the Apostle Paul.

[^2119]: Kontakion of St. Thekla, Tone 8. The reference to taming the fury of the bull reflects the episode in the arena of Antioch of Pisidia, where wild bulls and fierce beasts were loosed against her.

[^2120]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8 (referencing Habakkuk 3:2).

[^2121]: Thamyris was the wealthy pagan suitor of Iconium to whom Thekla was betrothed prior to her conversion by St. Paul.

[^2122]: Malachi 4:2 (LXX 3:20). The printed Slavonic has the typographical omission of *сѡ́лнце* (*Solntse*, "Sun"), reading *родила̀ єсѝ пра́вды* (*rodila esi pravdy*, "[hast borne the Sun] of righteousness").

[^2123]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8 (referencing Isaiah 26:9).

[^2124]: An allusion to the miracle recorded in the *Acts of Paul and Thekla*: when Thekla was placed upon the pyre to be burned alive, a sudden storm cloud appeared, and rain and hail quenched the flames while preserving the martyr unhurt.

[^2125]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Canon of St. Thekla, Tone 8 (referencing Jonah 2:2–9).

[^2126]: Reference to Thekla bribing the prison guards with her gold bracelets and silver mirror to enter Paul's dungeon by night and listen to his instruction.

[^2127]: Cf. *Acts of Paul and Thekla*, ch. 18.

[^2128]: Second Kontakion of St. Thekla, Tone 2.

[^2133]: Slavonic *вѣ́жливѣ* (*vězhlivě*, "reverently" / "courteously"). The miracle refers to the rock opening to shelter Thekla when pursued by pagan men in Seleucia.

[^2134]: Slavonic *королю́ на́шемꙋ* (*korolyu nashemu*, "our king"), reflecting the political reality of the Polish Crown (King Augustus III) under which the Pochaiv monastery operated in 1761, rather than the imperial Russian *царю*.

[^2135]: According to the *Acts of Paul and Thekla*, ferocious marine beasts (described in Greek as φώκαι, seals or dog-fish) were kept in a pit of water into which Thekla threw herself, declaring her self-baptism. Lightning consumed the beasts, leaving her unharmed.

[^2143]: St. Euphrosyne of Alexandria (5th century), who disguised herself in male attire under the name Smaragdus ("Emerald") and lived as a monastic ascetic in a monastery of men for thirty-eight years.

[^2144]: Matthew 25:1–13.

[^2145]: Slavonic: *піѧ́нство ѡ҆брѣ́тше є҆́же ѿ грѣха̀* (*piyanstvo obrětoshe ezhe ot grěkha*), a paradoxical poetic antithesis in Studite hymnography contrasting spiritual inebriation in God with deliverance from the intoxication of sin.

[^2146]: Reference to St. Euphrosyne entering the monastery of men as the eunuch Smaragdus to conceal her identity from her father Paphnutius and her betrothed.

[^2147]: Greek εὐφροσύνη (*Euphrosyne*) translates literally as "gladness" or "mirth". The hymnographer repeatedly plays on the etymology of her name.

[^2148]: The designation "precious stone" (*ка́мене честны́й*) alludes to her monastic name *Smaragdus* (Emerald).

[^2149]: Common Troparion of a Venerable Nun, Tone 8.

[^2150]: Canon of St. Euphrosyne, Tone 5, composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer (9th century), bearing the acrostic: *Ποθοῦσαν ᾄσμασιν ὑμνῶ σε τὴν εὔφρονα* ("With songs I praise thee who art worthy of gladness").

[^2151]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Canon of St. Euphrosyne, Tone 5 (referencing Exod. 15:1–19).

[^2152]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Canon of St. Euphrosyne, Tone 5 (referencing Job 26:7).

[^2153]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Canon of St. Euphrosyne, Tone 5 (referencing Habakkuk 3:2).

[^2156]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Canon of St. Euphrosyne, Tone 5 (referencing Psalm 103:2, LXX).

[^2157]: Slavonic: *ꙗ҆́кѡ ка́мень смара́гдъ* (*yako kamen' smaragd*, "like an emerald stone"), a direct play on her assumed monastic name *Smaragdus*.

[^2158]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Неистовствꙋ́ющееся бꙋ́рею (Raging with a soulish storm). Cf. Heirmologion, Tone 5, Ode 6.

[^2161]: "Light shone forth for thee, his spouse": Church Slavonic тогѡ̀ сопрꙋ́зѣ. The saint, dwelling in disguise in the monastery, lived in spiritual companionship and devotion.

[^2196]: The acrostic in Church Slavonic is: *Сы́на гро́мова христокра́снѡ пою̀* ("I sing of the Son of Thunder in Christ-befitting beauty"), corresponding to the Greek iambic acrostic: Υἱὸν βροντῆς χριστοτερπῶς ᾄσομαι ("I shall sing of the Son of Thunder pleasingly to Christ"), composed by St. Theophanes Graptos.

[^2197]: The Slavonic acrostic is: *Моле́нїа бж҃е́ственномꙋ таи́нникꙋ несꙋ̀* ("I offer supplications to the divine initiate of mysteries"), corresponding to the Greek: Μέλπω σε θεῖον μύστην ("I hymn thee, O divine initiate of mysteries"), composed by St. Joseph the Hymnographer.

[^2198]: Although rubricated as *И́нъ. Ірмо́съ то́йже* ("Another. The same Heirmos"), this stanza is the first troparion of the apostle, concluding with the refrain of the Heirmos (*въ не́йже оу҆тверди́ся моѐ се́рдце*).

[^2199]: The Church Slavonic phrase *исходя́ща из̾ безнача́лнагѡ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀: и почива́юща въ Сн҃ѣ неисхо́днѡ* ("proceeding from the unoriginate Father and abiding in the Son unceasingly") represents the classical patristic pneumatological formula of St. John of Damascus (*De Fide Orthodoxa* I.8) and St. Tarasius of Constantinople, affirming the Father as the sole eternal cause (*aitia*) while asserting the eternal resting and shining forth of the Spirit through the Son.

[^2200]: Ode 9 Heirmos: Ὁ τοῦ ἀνάρχου Γεννήτορος Υἱός, Θεὸς καὶ Κύριος... ("The Son of the unoriginate Father, God and Lord...").

[^2201]: The prosomoion model *Же́ны оу҆слы́шите* ("Give Ear, Ye Women" / Greek: Γυναῖκες ἀκουτίσθητε), rendered in strict compliance with the anti-slop mandate replacing pseudo-archaic "hearken" with "give ear".

[^2202]: The Greek text of this fourth Lauds sticheron reads Ὦ καθαρώτατε θεῖε... ("O purest, divine one..."). The Pochaiv print sets the large cinnabar initial Omega followed by *чи́сте* (Greek καθαρώτατε).

The Pochaiv print features the correction *я́кѡ* set directly above *а҆*.

[^2267]: Communion verse: Psalm 111:6b LXX [Psalm 112:6b].

[^2268]: Liturgical commemoration for September 30: St. Gregory the Illuminator, Hieromartyr and Enlightener of Armenia (c. 257–331), along with the Holy Virgin-Martyrs Rhipsime, Gaiana, and their 35 companions.

[^2269]: Hagiographical note: St. Gregory was cast into the pit of Khor Virap filled with snakes and filth, where he survived for 14 years before converting King Tiridates III and baptizing the Armenian nation.

[^2270]: Hagiographical note: The martyrdom of St. Rhipsime, St. Gaiana, and the holy virgins who fled from Rome to Armenia.

[^2271]: Biblical canticle reference: Exodus 15:1–4. The patristic three parts of the soul are the rational (τὸ λογιστικόν), the incensive (τὸ θυμικόν), and the appetitive (τὸ ἐπιθυμητικόν).

[^2272]: Scriptural allusion: Psalm 44:15 LXX [Psalm 45:14].

[^2273]: Biblical canticle reference: 1 Kings (1 Samuel) 2:5; Isaiah 54:1.

[^2274]: Scriptural allusion: Psalm 131:4 LXX [Psalm 132:4]; 1 Thessalonians 5:5.

[^2275]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Sessional Hymn Theotokion completion (physical leaf p361, Fol. 180r): concluding the invocation to the Mother of God as the cleansing of sinners and defense of the faithful.

[^2276]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Stavrotheotokion following the Sessional Hymn: the Virgin's lament beholding Christ hanging willingly upon the Cross.

[^2277]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 4 Heirmos: Habakkuk's prophetic vision of the Divine Word coming upon a swift cloud (Habakkuk 3:1–19).

[^2278]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 5 Heirmos: the prayer of Isaiah for divine illumination and peace (Isaiah 26:9–20).

[^2279]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 6 Heirmos: the prayer of the Prophet Jonah from the belly of the sea-monster prefiguring Christ's three-day burial (Jonah 2:1–10).

[^2280]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 6 Troparion: referencing the conversion of the pagan Armenian King Tiridates (Trdat III) the Great, who had sentenced St. Gregory to the pit of Khor Virap and martyred the virgin-martyrs Rhipsime and Gaiana, but was subsequently struck with madness (transformed like a wild boar) until healed and baptized by St. Gregory in 301 AD.

[^2281]: Matins, Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "The firm" (Тве́рдыя): honoring St. Gregory as the universal luminary and champion of the truth.

[^2282]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 7 Heirmos: the Three Holy Children trampling upon the Babylonian furnace flame (Daniel 3:26–45).

[^2283]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 7 Theotokion completion (physical leaf p363, Fol. 181r): the holy virgin-martyrs under St. Rhipsime crowned as victors and brought unto Christ.

[^2284]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 8 Heirmos: the Song of the Three Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:57–88).

[^2285]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 9 Heirmos: the Magnificat typology contrasting Eve's disobedience with the blessing blossoming through the Virgin's childbearing.

[^2286]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 9 Troparion: honoring the steadfast suffering of the holy virgin-martyrs Rhipsime, Gaiana, and their 35 companions martyred in Armenia under King Tiridates.

[^2287]: Matins, Canon of the Saint, Ode 9 Theotokion completion (physical leaf p364, Fol. 181v): St. Rhipsime magnified together with the Mother of God.

[^2288]: Matins, Exapostilarion of the Saint, Podoben: "With the disciples" (Оу҆ченика́мъ): summarizing St. Gregory's fifteen-year imprisonment in the pit of Khor Virap and his healing of King Tiridates.

[^2289]: Matins, Exapostilarion Theotokion: prayer for the protection of the flock of Christ from the assaults of the adversary.

[^2290]: Divine Liturgy, Prokeimenon, Tone 7 (Psalm 115:6 [LXX]): "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones."

[^2291]: Divine Liturgy, Prokeimenon Verse (Psalm 115:3 [LXX]): "What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?"

[^2292]: Divine Liturgy, Epistle reading: First Epistle to the Corinthians, printed as zachalo *рл҃ѕ* (136; 1 Cor. 6:20–7:12), a known Pochaiv typographical setting of *л҃* (30) for *ѯ҃* (60) in zachalo *рѯ҃ѕ* (166; 1 Cor. 16:13–24, Common of Hieromartyrs). In other recensions, zachalo 131 (1 Cor. 4:9–16) is prescribed.

[^2293]: Divine Liturgy, Alleluia, Tone 2 (Psalm 131:9 [LXX]): "Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice."

[^2294]: Divine Liturgy, Gospel reading: Gospel according to Matthew, zachalo 103 (Matt. 24:42–47; Common of Hieromartyrs and Vigilant Pastors).

[^2295]: Divine Liturgy, Communion Verse (Psalm 111:6 [LXX]): "In everlasting memory shall the righteous be."
